## Disenchanter

Disenchanters were dromedary-like creatures that fed on the dweomer of magic items.

Description
Disenchanters looked roughly like spindly, pale electric blue dromedaries. Their small heads possessed a flexible, elephant-like trunk which could reach out . The creatures were somewhat translucent, and would occasionally shimmer.In the 3rd edition Fiend Folio, while retaining many of their traits from prior writeups, disenchanters are presented differently in key ways that can't be reconciled with their Forgotten Realms-specific description in Pages from the Mages, which is in line with that of the 1st edition Fiend Folio. This article therefore ignores the physical description from this source per the Canon Hierarchy, despite the fact that it is more recent.

Abilities
The disenchanter's namesake ability allowed them to leech the dweomer of any magic item touched by its snout, rendering the tapped object fully intact but magically inert. This ability could also be used to absorb tangible spell effects.

In order to support their diet, disenchanters could detect magic around them in a  radius.

Behavior
Typically docile creatures, disenchanters tended to become aggressive only towards those carrying magical items. Despite the fact that they were more difficult to drain, the creatures had a preference for objects with stronger enchantments, although they avoided extremely potent items, such as artifacts.

Disenchanters could speak, but typically only in their own language. A scant few were known to speak Common or Elven.

Combat
Disenchanters could only be struck by magical weapons, which did not lose their capabilities from coming into contact with any part of the body besides the snout.

Ecology

An obscure beast, the disenchanter's curious metabolism was poorly understood, and its lairing conditions were a mystery. Similarly, the reproductive cycle of the creature was unknown, though the lack of evidence of young specimens led many sages to believe the creatures were born through fission when a disenchanter fed on enough magic. Upon death, a disenchanter's body shriveled up into a small withered gray lump.

Disenchanters had no known predators, which led some sages to conclude that their population was tied to the availability of magical energy in their environment.

Habitats
Disenchanters usually lived in the lands east of Anauroch, with most of the creatures taking up residence around the Sea of Fallen Stars.

Uses
A disenchanter's cranial fluids were used in the concoction of a disorienting and corrosive poison known as calad.

 of disenchanter hair was typically used in the creation of a bottle of oil of disenchantment.

A technique to improve one's chances with a great elixir was to drink any other beneficial elixir immediately followed up by an entire goblet of disenchanter blood. Doing so got rid of the positive effects of the "lesser" elixir, but in return almost eliminated the danger of death from drinking the great elixir.

The essence of a disenchanter was reportedly effective when used in ink for writing scrolls. It was also possibly used in creating rods of cancellation.

By the, disenchanter brains were among the items considered to be a possible universal material component.

History
Some sages speculated that disenchanters were originally created by the phaerimm as a weapon against Netheril or the sharn.

Every 150 years or so, disenchanters converged upon the historic site of Sarbreen, which since the 13 century housed the city of Ravens Bluff. The last of these Plagues of Disenchanters before the settlement of the Living City, around the, was witnessed by the evoker Tym, whose writings about the creatures in A Miscellany of Magical Monsters led to more widespread knowledge about disenchanters.

Sometime in the 1360s DR, the Emerald Enclave member Selsian Earthenhands started off his ill-fated project to construct a garden near the Elven Court in Cormanthor, commissioned by his organization to welcome migrating Evermeetian elves in return for favors from Queen Amlaruil Moonflower, by releasing a pack of disenchanters to clear out undesired ambient magic from the site.

The first Plague of Disenchanters experienced by Ravens Bluff circa the, drew mixed reactions. The Chauntean House of Bountiful Harvest saw the creatures as a blessing from their goddess, and decreed no harm should come to them. Conversely, the Wizards Guild put up a bounty of 100 gp per disenchanter tail, which caused a rapid decrease in the creatures' numbers in the area. The Guild retracted this bounty not too long after they deemed the danger was quelled.

Notable Disenchanters
In the mid-14 century DR, the Tsurlagoli slaver Temmi Dharimm employed a number of trained monsters, including a pair of disenchanters.

## Diary of Damien Morienus

The Diary of Damien Morienus was the journal of necromancer Damien Morienus, former Overwizard of the Host Tower of the Arcane.

Description
It was a black leather-clad old book with an embroidered "M" on its cover, which stood for the name of the diary's owner. The diary was old but readable, after spending a century in the dark and damp underground. The book was about  by, and  in thickness.

Contents
The Diary of Damien Morienus was written in an arcane script that could be deciphered by a magic-user or read using a read magic spell.

The diary itself contained copious notes and theories on the nature of life and death, the necromancer's research into the power of lichdom, formulas, spells, and recipes. Most importantly, the diary contained an incomplete recipe for a magic potion that would, if the research was finished, turn the drinker into a lich.

History
The Diary of Damien Morienus was the bounty sought after by evil wizards of the Host Tower of the Arcane Celerum the Black and Kaladar the Cruel in 1369 DR.

The Diary was still in Damien's possession circa 1269 DR when the Accursed Tower of the necromancer was swallowed by the mud of the Great Thaw. The inhabitants of the tower perished and were raised as undead because of the curse of one of Myrkul's clerics. Damien himself was transformed into a crypt thing who was clutching the diary when a group of adventurers discovered the tower in search of the diary.

## Display

A display was a secondary effect of the manifestation of a psionic power. Several categories of display were known: auditory, material, mental, olfactory, and visual, though many powers had their own displays specific to them.

Effects
A display occurred in addition to the intended primary effect of a power. Most powers had a display of some kind, and some could have multiple displays, though they typically had only one or two, while a few had none. However, while manifesting their powers, any manifester could choose to suppress all displays through an act of concentration; this was harder for higher-level powers and failure would see the displays appear as normal. Dismissing a power also caused a display. A witness skilled in psicraft could identify a power from the display it produced.

Displays were minor enough to have no hindrance in battle or daily life, but they were noticeable as the flashy special effects of psionics use, though users of dimension door might augment its visual display to cause a blinding flash to dazzle onlookers or a few kineticists might learn to enhance their energy powers to produce spectacular auditory or visual displays that could dazzle a target. Multiple displays occurring at once were not cumulative, but would briefly increase in intensity before returning to a normal level. From this, one skilled in psicraft could determine the number of active powers in an area.

Types
Auditory
A standard auditory display was a bass hum sounding similar to many deep voices heard all at once. Within a second, it quickly rose in volume from barely audible to a shout loud enough to be heard  away. The manifester could suppress this to a soft noise one might struggle to hear within only . The sound was centered either at the manifester or at target of the power, as the manifester chose.

Material
A standard material display saw the target or area momentarily covered in a sheen of ectoplasm, glistening or shimmering but translucent in appearance. This was a seepage from Astral Plane. It was inert and evaporated in several seconds.

Mental
A standard mental display was a subtle chime sensed in the minds of those nearby; this normally rang once, but it could ring as long as the power lasted if the manifester wished. It could be "heard" up to  and was centered either at the manifester or at target of the power, as the manifester chose.

Olfactory
A standard olfactory display produced a peculiar odor. No one could specify it exactly and everyone described it differently; the same display might smell like honeysuckle to one witness and burning wood to another. In any case, it was strangely familiar and prompted an old memory to be briefly recalled. Beginning at the manifester, the odor spread for  in each direction, but dissipated in under a second, though it could remain as long as the power lasted if the manifester wished.

Visual
A standard visual display had two effects. First, in all cases, the manifester's eyes glowed with silver fire for the duration of the power. Second, a flash of light of rainbow hue burst from them but dissipated only  away, unless the power had its own specific visual display or created something visible.

Appendix
Backgrounds
These set displays for psionic powers are specific to 3 and 3.5-edition psionics, and do not have a specific counterpart in other editions, where powers may or may not have descriptions of their effects.

References

---

## Dilation

The dilation group of spells were alteration spells used by wizards.

Effects
Dilation I and dilation II allowed the caster to increase the area of effect for the spell cast immediately after. If more than a minute passed before the second spell was cast then the dilation spell was wasted.

Dilation I increased the area of effect of basic spells by a quarter. Dilation II increased the area of effect of basic spells by half and intermediate spells by a quarter.

Components
These spells only required verbal components to cast.

## Divine Fury

Divine Fury was the name given to a number of magical katanas that appeared in northwest Faerûn circa 1372 DR.

History
Although unconfirmed, in the account of Kaa Mihnd, these swords were forged from fallen stars that crashed into the square of a poor fishing village somewhere. Kaa Mihnd and the other villagers used these swords to rise up against their rich overlord and reclaim their ancestral land.

These katanas bore either a wide guard or a brown cloth wrapped around the lower blade.

Disease was a mummy monk who roamed the Lowerdark of the Underdark with her companions, the dread wraith Death and the undead beholder Destruction.

History
Hamezaar, the vampire ruler of the Boneyard, once worked to recruit Death, Disease, and Destruction to his undead armies, but they declined for lack of interest.

Disappearing ink was used, often by more sophisticated thieves, when one wanted their written word to be less than binding.

## Diakk

Diakka, also sometimes known as diakkas, were avian creatures native to all the layers of the plane known as Hades.

Description
There were two distinct groups of diakk, the varath and the carcene. Both varieties had human-like heads and and human-like arms in place of wings. These arms were rather small and clawed.

The varath were taller and stork-like, their vaguely human faces sported long bills, and their arms were thin. They also gave out loon-like cries, leading them to be dubbed "screechers" in the cant of Sigil. Meanwhile, the carcene were short and broad, resembling more of a squat pelican with a foreshortened bill. Unlike the varath, their cries were more in the vein of gobbling croaks and caws.

The color of the feathers on a diakk's body varied wildly, often featuring combinations of colors and in sickening hues. Typical colors included pea-green, rusty-red orange, purple, orange, pink, and dirty yellow. Their bills, claws, feet, and legs were typically either black, dark brown, maroon, or deep green in color. Their human-like heads were feathered, though their faces were featherless and brightly colored, being a color similar to their oily plumage.

Personality
Unlike the plane's most common inhabitants, the hordlings, diakka were far more organized and disliked erratic behavior. Though they still were not that bright, but Carcene were slightly more intelligent than varath.

All diakka were known to be very cowardly, devious, and sneaky. There was no mutual care or respect among them and a diakk would never put itself in jeopardy for another.

Abilities
These creatures had innate magical abilities that allowed them to cast the spells audible glamer once per day, jump twice per day, weakness once per day. If at least four of them encircled a group of enemies, they were capable of performing a magical dance of weakness that envoked a hypnotic area of effect similar to the spell ray of enfeeblement, that would effect creatures regardless of their magic resistance.

Combat
Diakka always attacked intelligently. The strongest member of any flock would assess opponents for both intent and weakness. Diakka would then attack only if they assessed a foe as being weaker than them. Powerful, hostile creatures would only be attacked if appeasement can not be reached.

When confronted with obviously strong opponents the diakka would work to surround their enemy, forcing them into a compact group, and use audible glamer to make them believe more diakka were on the way. The largest diakk would bound in and out of melee range of their opponents using the spell jump, while using the spell weakness to supplement their savage strikes.

Vartah preferred to stab at enemies with their long, sharp bills while the squat carcene preferred to tear at them with their clawed arms.

Society
Much like birds, these creatures were organized in flocks that comprised both types of diakk. There were generally an equal number of each type, varath were sometimes more numerous. One or sometimes two diakka acted as the leader of a flock, a position that was often taken up by carcene.

Diet
The diakk was a carnivorous species, willing to eat the flesh of almost anything weaker than themselves.

Languages
In addition to speaking their own language, diakka spoke the language of many creatures found in the Lower planes.

Relationships
Hordlings and other denizens of Hades were often known to feast upon these creatures. Hordlings considered them a staple in their diet.

Night hags were often known to summon these creatures to the Prime Material plane and use them as guards or servants, but eventually put them in their stew pots to be eaten. Those within Hades used them to guard their soul larvae. Their relationship with night hags was one of mutual hatred, though both believed it to be to their benefit.

A number of diakka served Baba Yaga and patrolled her magical dancing hut. It was thought by some that the species was originally created by this archfey, supposedly through the transformation of chickens she kept when she lived on a farm. These diakka saw Baba Yaga as their mother.

In addition, diakka were often seen acting as the servants of daemons throughout Hades.

## Diabolus

Diaboli (singular: Diabolus) were a race of people native to the Demiplane of Nightmares, where they filled the same niche humans did on the Material Plane.

Description
Diaboli were vaguely humanoid in form, averaging about  tall, but possessed goat-like legs, pointed ears, and small, vestigial horns. They also had long tails that ended in a barb. Their hands were mostly normal but they lacked the smallest finger, instead only having three fingers and a thumb on each hand. Diaboli had reptilian eyes that ranged from bright red to yellow in color. Their skin ranged in color from pale mauve or lavender to near-black violet. They had forked tongues. Diaboli varied in how much hair grew on their bodies: bare diaboli had no hair at all, common diaboli had more normal amounts of hair, and hirsute diaboli also had coarse, goat-like hair growing from the waist down.

Abilities
Diaboli possessed darkvision. Additionally, their forked tongues provided an enhanced sense of smell and some sense of heat detection.

According to some sources, diaboli were not affected by magic cast by creatures from the Material plane, and diaboli magic likewise did not affect creatures of the Material plane. However, indirect uses of magic could be wielded against them, and magic used by other creatures from the Demiplane of Nightmares, such as neh-thalggu, feyrs, nagpas, and maelephants, still affected them.

Personality
Diaboli had a wide range of personalities among themselves, but by and large they tended to be rather chaotic, if benign; this manifested in the form of extreme absent-mindedness, mild to severe mood swings, wild bouts of creativity, and otherwise mercurial personalities. However, they strongly believed in the ancient traditions and mores that held their societies together.

Combat
Diaboli martial technology and techniques were not as advanced as those of the cultures of the Material plane, but most were skilled with the trident. Additionally, diaboli could attack with their tail barb, which was mildly poisonous.

Society
Diaboli were native to the Demiplane of Nightmares, a place where the Region of Dreams brushed up against the Far Realm, and the latter plane leaked through and tainted the place. Because of this, some diaboli fled through the Ethereal plane to Material planes such as Faerûn, where they settled and built small, isolated communities.

On both their native plane and in their other settlements, diaboli had no form of government, instead practicing a form of anarchy limited only by traditional customs, taboos, and a strong sense of fair play. They rejected all forms of government because they could not prove any one form of government superior to another.

Diaboli settlements were built in a strange, haphazard manner, without any apparent consideration for either defense or ease of navigation. The buildings themselves resembled ruins, with leaning stone walls and protruding beams and timbers. Various colors of paint in random splotches provided some form of camouflage from a distance.

Diaboli societies spared no amount of either wealth or energy on developing either philosophy or art. They were very generous and tolerant among themselves. However, diaboli innately feared humans and most other humanoids, and viewed humans as hideous demons or devils. Communication with others never went further than cool politeness.
Language
Diaboli spoke their own language, called Diabolan. This language made frequent use of harsh, guttural hisses and the unique physiology of their forked tongues, making it difficult for most humanoids of the Material plane to learn. Most words contained soft "f", "s", "th", and "z" sounds, among other, stranger ones. However, the same physiology that made it difficult for humanoids to learn Diabolan also made it difficult for diaboli to learn Common, and even those that did manage spoke it with a thick, serpentine accent. Typically, a diabolus might learn Abyssal, Common, Draconic, or Infernal. Additionally, diaboli had a tail code, a method of communicating simple ideas through the positioning and twirling of their tails.
Religion
Diaboli had no established religion, instead revering ideas such as anarchy, chaos, freedom, and wonder. Clerics existed, though they were very rare.
Relationships
The neh-thalggu occupied a place in diabolus legends similar to the one dragons held in human legends. Diaboli regarded neh-thalggu as creatures of great power, cunning, and inscrutability. Additionally, neh-thalggu were some of the few creatures whose magic could affect a diabolus.

In the Mystaran nation of Glantri, those who could trace their lineage back to diaboli were afforded a higher social status.

## Dire spider

Dire spiders were bigger, tougher, and smarter relatives of regular giant spiders that were not considered to be true dire creatures.

Description
Dire spiders varied in size and color. Most commonly they were tan or dark brown in color and could be differentiated from their giant cousins by being bigger. Very seldomly, a dire spider could reach even greater size, much bigger than a usual humanoid creature, these creatures were referred to as bloated dire spiders.

Behavior
They were cunning ambushers and trap makers and were proficient in hiding and waiting for their prey, once the creature was touching their webs, they attacked. Dire spiders hunted in groups.

Abilities
Dire spiders' abilities were similar to most other spiderkind, they possessed darkvision and were immune to mind-affecting abilities and spells. They were also unaffected by any magically created webs.

Combat
When they ambushed their prey, they charged into close combat, confident in their size, strength, and numbers. They dealt slashing damage with their sharp tarsus. Like many other spiders, dire spiders were highly poisonous. When in close combat, dire spiders attempted to inject their victims with their venom upon a bite.

Ecology
Dire spider venom was potent and was of a similar type as other huge spiders'. Their venom affected the injected creature's strength and if remained untreated, dammed it even further, potentially enough to kill physically weaker creatures or humanoids with lower fortitude.

Dire spiders were notoriously next to impossible to train nor domesticate due to their ferocity.

Summoning
One could summon a dire spider via the summon monster IV spell.

History
Circa 1372 DR, dire spiders could be found in the sewers of Neverwinter, especially in the city's poor quarters, such as the Beggar's Nest and inside the Silver Sails Trading Company storehouse in the Docks District. More of these creatures could be found within Neverwinter Woods, spider-infested areas of Underdark, sewers under Daggerford and Spindrift Town., as well as the depths of the Brush of Barbs.

Duskwood and Glowstone Caverns under it, had a significant population of dire spiders circa 1374 DR.

Rumors & Legends
Rumors claimed that a dire spider was kept inside the Neverwinter Academy.

## Diyab

Diyab was a divine spellcaster and devotee of the Bhaalspawn Sarevok who lived in the Realms during the mid−14 century DR.

Activities
Diyab worked under Zhalimar Cloudwulfe alongside his fellow agents Aasim, Alai, Gardush, and Naaman. Together they sought to advance Sarevok's plans and goals over those of the Iron Throne.

Possessions
He wielded a +1 warhammer and carried with him a number of potions and oils.

History
In the, Diyab and his allies were sent by the Iron Throne to the city of Baldur's Gate to advise Thaldorn in his negotiations with Emissary Tar, who represented the Baldurian Council of Four. Right before the meeting, the Diyab and the others were defeated by Abdel Adrian and his companions.

## Dire badger

Dire badgers, also known as giant badgers, were bigger, tougher, smarter cousins of regular badgers. There were known to be animal companions of rangers, druids, and gnome cavaliers.

Description
These short and tough creatures had thick brown fur, short legs, and dangerously sharp claws. Their pointed faces had a mouth of sharp teeth.

Behavior
Dire badgers tended to live in solitude or cetes of up to five animals. They were fiercely territorial in the wild. They laired underground in the tunnels they burrowed. Their tough claws could dig through most things but rocks and the tunnels' diameter was around .

Combat
In combat, dire badgers became a fierce ball of claws and teeth, attacking their opponents relentlessly. Being hurt in battle sent them into a berserk rage and they fought even more ferociously. The rage only ended when the danger had passed or either the opponent or the dire badger was dead. They were territorial and fiercely defended their territory.

Ecology
Dire badgers were known to be tamed and trained to serve as mounts. Gnomes used trained dire badgers for their elite cavalry, aided by the riders having the ability to speak with animals once a day. A dire badger youth could cost around 100 gp with additional 15 gp to pay for their training. Upkeep of a domesticated or trained badger could come to up to 8 gp per month.

Wild dire badgers could be found in the Forest of Amtar of Dambrath, as well as in the lands of Unther.

Dire badgers were one of the animals that could be summoned via the summon creature I spell.

Uses
Gnomes occasionally used dire badgers as guard animals.

History
In 1374 DR, the Cult of Tiamat in Unther fed dire badgers and other dangerous creatures to Grelixtralithimon, a redspawn birther.

Notable Dire Badgers
Zulkir was the awakened dire badger companion of Ryngoth, a member of the Cult of the Dragon in the ruins of Myth Drannor circa 1369 DR.

## Dirbert

Dirbert was a young man who lived in Imnesvale during the mid–14 century DR.

Personality
Dirbert was rather naive and impetuous, and did not thoroughly think through his actions. Likes his peers, Dirbert wanted to become a daring adventurer.

Relationships
Dirbert was friends with Imnesvale's other youths, namely Neler and Valsben.

History
In the, Dirbert and his friends went around town looking for anyone that would buy them some bastard swords and a keg of De'Tranion's Baalor ale, so they could begin their adventuring career in earnest.

## Diccon Picard

Diccon Picard was a jeweler of the town of Ghars in Cormyr in the mid–14 century DR.

Activities
He ran a jewellery shop on Wattle Lane in Ghars. It was secured with a heavy wooden door with steel bands.

When not working or with a customer, Diccon liked to play his fiddle, knowing such songs as "Warrior's Woe" and "Red-haired Lad". However, he was no great talent at it.

Personality

A natural salesman, Diccon flattered all his potential customers, even those who couldn't afford to buy.

Description
His hair was snow white, big and bushy.

History
On Kythorn 27 in the, Diccon Picard and the merchant Barthelm Meadowbrock rode out of town one evening to see what the new Swamp Rat (Diccon misremembered it as Swamp Fox) tavern was like. They weren't impressed. On the way back, they sighted the ghost of the bandit chieftain Fastred and raced back to town in fear. Since Barthelm didn't want to lose business with Shortshanks of the rival Bold Bard tavern, Diccon let on that he had been alone.

Later that year, on Eleint 18, Jasper went to Diccon's jewellery store to ask him about the ghost sighting, after it was discovered to be Dovo in disguise.

## Didaji

Sheikh Didaji was a leader of one of the Bedine tribes that lived in Anauroch during the mid–14 century DR.

Description
He was a rather tall man, with sunken, angular features. Due to the fact the men of his tribe covered their faces rather than the women, Didaji wore a scarf around his face that he wore along with the typical turban.

Relationships
Uncharacteristically of a Bedine sheikh, Didaji was wholly supportive of the sorceress Ruha in the use of her arcane powers. Unlike others he felt it was a blessing from the gods, rather than a curse.

History
Didaji and his tribe's warriors were among those present during the final battles between the Bedine people and the Zhentilar army that took place in the . They helped route the asabi mercenaries from their tunnel system in the south of the Wall of Fallen Djinn mountain range in the Battle of the Fissures, and served as a reserve force alongside the Raz'hadi during the siege of Orofin.

## Discord (sword)

Discord was a bastard sword rumored to have been enchanted with the aid of planar beings.

Description
This exotic sword had a line of black crystals embedded along the blade.

Powers
Like other weapons created by Ortus, Discord started resonating with unnatural frequency upon hitting its target. The sound creating by this was virtually imperceptible by mortal ears, though they suffered its effects regardless—it dealt sonic damage and had a good chance of causing confusion. Additionally, this bastard sword possessed a +2 enchantment.

History
Discord's creator was known as Ortus of Gond, the Mad Tinker. It was a mystery where the uncanny black crystals came from; some believed they were of planar origins, as Ortus was known to have offered his crafting services to beings from beyond the Prime Material plane.

Circa 1374 DR, Discord was in possession of one of the Cult of the Dragon lackeys who served under Kaela Ulsant in Thentia, Moonsea.

## Discipliner

Discipliners were cursed rings fashioned after the rings of folly in Narfell.

Description
Discipliners were metallic bands with a round hollow circle in its center, reminiscent of a ring with a missing impeded gemstone.

Powers
Just like rings of folly, Discipliners magically and drastically reduced the wearer's natural intelligence and wisdom, rendering them drooling idiots and affected them with the feeblemind spell. These rings could only be removed with the remove curse spell, or by a capable cleric.

History
Discipliners were created by Hergal Norin of Narfell. The wizard used these rings to discipline his most egotistical students. He presented a Discipliner to a student for their "obvious brilliance" to teach them a lesson in humility.

In 1368 DR, several of these rings could be found on the Sword Coast. A mad sage Arcand sought to recover a Discipliner from a certain shipwreck to the south from Baldur's Gate for one deluded reason or the other. Another ring was in the lair of the cursed spider-woman of the Cloak Wood, Centeol. And lastly, one more of these enchanted rings was in possession of a djinni merchant Khan Zahraa in Trademeet on a hunt for a rakshasa. Khan was ready to "reward" an unhelpful adventurer with the cursed ring.

Abilities
Like all the other gnomes in Gnomengarde, Dimble had some training as a wizard in addition to his innate abilities. In combat, he preferred to attack with spells like ray of frost and magic missile.

## Divine bloodline

Divine bloodline was a divine divination spell used by specialty priests devoted to Siamorphe called Highborns, enabling them to determine noble lineage.

Effects
This spell allowed the caster to determine noble blood within the target and how closely related they were to a known noble, including bloodlines long forgotten in history. The caster's skill determined how far back along the lineage they could trace and the number of bloodlines present for any given target.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components the priest's holy symbol to cast.

## Divine investiture

Divine investiture was a divine enchantment and illusion spell used by specialty priests devoted to Siamorphe called Highborns during important noble ceremonies.

Effects
This spell was used on newborn noble heirs, during coronations, or upon receipt of a noble title as determined by the divine bloodline spell. The target was bathed in radiant light the color of which was selected by the caster. Pageantry illusions appeared that were appropriate for the event. The recipient received the legacy of Siamorphe which varied from person to person, but usually granted a small permanent boon to the recipient.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material components a chalice of holy water and dye laced with gold flakes to cast. The dye was used to trace the target's heraldry upon their body before the spell was cast. The holy water was poured over the dye during the casting, making it vanish.

## Divine Contention

Divine Contention is an online-only digital adventure that was released as additional content for the Dungeons & Dragons Essentials Kit for the 5th edition of Dungeons & Dragons. It is the third and final adventure in a trilogy called Beyond the Dragon of Icespire Peak, a sequel adventure to Dragon of Icespire Peak, and continues the story from Sleeping Dragon's Wake.

Distribution
Originally, the Beyond the Dragon of Icespire Peak trilogy (Storm Lord's Wrath, Sleeping Dragon's Wake, and Divine Contention) was available exclusively through D&D Beyond, either as standalone purchases or for free with purchase of a physical copy of the Dungeons & Dragons Essentials Kit. The product was subsequently made available for various virtual tabletop platforms.

## Distance distortion

Distance distortion was a common alteration spell.

Effects
When cast, this spell modified the area of an enclosed space. Since the spell was Earth-based, the enclosed area had to be surrounded by rock, sand, or soil. For the spell to be successful, the caster first had to cast conjure elemental to summon an earth elemental. The caster then must announce their intention to cast an distance distortion spell to the newly summoned earth elemental, to prevent the earth elemental from fighting the caster or attempting to break its summoning.

The spell used the earth elemental to increase or decrease, depending on the needs of the caster, the area of the targeted enclosed space. The magically modified area was not detectable by mundane means but a true seeing spell was able to detect the faint magical aura radiating from the modified space. Once the spell was completed, the earth elemental returned to its home plane. The spell could not be cast while in the phlogiston.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material component, a small amount of soft clay.

History
Distance distortion was invented in  by the Netherese arcanist Fjord with the name Fjord's distortion.

## Digester

Digesters were a ravenous, predatory species of magical beasts.

Description
These creatures stood on average  in height upon two powerful hind legs and weighed around . Their bodies sported a long tail, had a grey pebbly hide with dagger-like markings that helped them to camouflaged, and overall measured  in length. Their heads were narrow with sucking mouths and sported a tube-like orifice on their forehead.

Behavior
Digesters had a ravenous appetite, rarely feeling full, but when they were the beasts laid low and tried to avoid other creatures.

Abilities
Digesters were capable of seeing in both areas of low-light and total darkness. They were also impervious to harm from acid.

These creatures were capable of shooting acid from the tube-like they sported on their foreheads, either spraying it out in a cone of roughly  or as a concentrated stream out to . Once a digester used this function they had to wait some time before they could spray again.

Combat
When a digester spotted a likely target they would charge at them and spray their acid. If they found that was not sufficient to kill their prey, a digester would then attack with their hind legs until they were able to use their acid spray once more.

History
Around 1372 DR, a digester roamed the valley of High Thorog after having been freed from Telkoun's Tower.

A year later, it was found that Quon's shop in Zhentil Keep held a digester in its kennels.

Ecology
Habitats

Digesters were typically found to inhabit forests within warm climates.

In the Shining South region of south Faerûn, packs of these creatures inhabited the Lluirwood, the Shaareach Forest, and the northern Mhair Jungles. In interior Faerûn, they could be found in the Vast Swamp.

## Dispelling breath

Dispelling breath was a spell used by dragons that allowed them to counter magic by using their breath.

Effects
Upon casting the spell, anyone who could not avoid the worst of the dragon's breath would be targeted by a force that would strip all their ongoing magical effects, much like a dispel magic targeting them directly. Area spells were automatically targeted if the breath encompassed the spot on which they were centered. The mage's power that could be brought to bear to try and counter was intermediate between dispel magic and greater dispel magic.

Components
Besides its somatic component, dispelling breath required a breath weapon to be used alongside the casting.

## Divine Den

The Divine Den was a temple of Malar in the small village of Bezentil, considered to be the most holy sites of worship for the Beastlord in all the Realms. It was considered the center of the Malarite faith for the entirety of the Eastern Heartlands and eastern lands north of Thay.

Activities
Clergy of the Divine Den would lead great hunting expeditions to the more exotic and dangerous locales of Faerûn in order to hunt down its most exotic prey.

Despite the belief there were few skilled smiths in Bezentil, twelve Malarite clergy members in the Divine Den were among the few that could forge the True Talons of the God, specialized Claws of Malar that were enchanted and doused in the blood of beasts that were killed during their hunts.

## Dispel exhaustion

Dispel exhaustion was an arcane illusion spell that convinced others that they were well-rested.

Effects
This spell affected up to four targets at once, allowing them to attack and move as if under the influence of the haste spell for one minute every ten minutes. This spell also temporarily healed half of any physical harm experienced before the spell was cast. This recovered health was lost before the target's actual health if the targets were harmed while the spell was active.

A second casting of dispel exhaustion on a target already under the influence of the same spell would negate both spells.

Components
This spell required only verbal and somatic components to cast.

History
This spell was found within ''Selvar's Ineffable Conjurations, Magicks, and Phantasms.

## Dimensional door

Dimensional door was a psionic devotion of the psychoportation discipline that allowed the psionicist to open a portal leading to another dimension.

Effects
The human-sized shimmering portal created by this power allowed the psionicist to step through and appear anywhere within  through another portal. The portals would remain as long as the psionicist expended psychic energy. The portals could only be used from a single side, allowing up to five individuals to step through at a time.

Stepping through a dimensional door was disorienting, dazing travelers and preventing them from performing complex tasks for a minute after traveling.

Notable Users
Individuals
 Vhondryl, a psionicist in Skullport and head of the Hall of Mental Splendor

Appendix
Appearances

## Dimension walk

Dimension walk was a psionic devotion of the psychoportation discipline that allowed the psionicist to travel through a portal to another dimension.

Effects
The portal opened with this power pierced other dimensions at right angles, making it better than the dimensional door power in several ways. Perhaps the most important was that traveling by dimension walk was not disorienting in any way. Secondly, the range of dimension walking was much greater, allowing the psionicist to travel up to  every ten minutes. The one drawback, aside from the travel not being instantaneous, was that only the psionicist was able to travel by this method since the portal closed right behind him, leaving any companions stranded.

Intense concentration was required on the psionicist's part while navigating the murky gray of the dimension. Slight distractions could mean that they would find themselves several miles off target when exiting the dimension at their destination.

Though the psionicist could bring their equipment with them, any magic items that held their own dimensions, such as a bag of holding, would spill their contents once they were within the dimension.

Users
Dimension walk was exhibited as an innate psionic ability by opinicuses.

Powerful shamans of the Great Worm tribe, who worshiped Uthgar's Great Worm beast totem, were capable of assuming a "great worm" form that conferred a number of psionic abilities, including dimension walk.

Known/Notable Users
 Elrem the Wise, a powerful Uthgar shaman of the Great Worm tribe.The North: Guide to the Savage Frontier and Powers & Pantheons both state that the "great worm" form an Uthgar shaman can shapechange into confers a number of psionic powers. The powers listed are nearly the same as those that were originally detailed for Elrem back in The Savage Frontier, thus it can be inferred that Elrem's psionic powers are not unique to him.
 Vhondryl, a psionicist in Skullport and head of the Hall of Mental Splendor

## Diminish breath weapon

Diminish breath weapon was a spell that was used against dragons.

Effects
This spell targeted the breath weapon of the targeted dragon. The spell significantly reduced the damage normally inflicted by said breath weapon.

Components
This spell only required a verbal component. The spell's strength could be increased if, during casting, the ashes of a valuable object were sprinkled by the caster. The object had to have been either damaged or destroyed by the breath weapon of the targeted dragon and had to have a value of over 100 gp.

## Diamond cure

Diamond Cure was a name given to a species of herb with antivenom properties that grew in Pelleor's Prairie.

Description
This herb looked much like a clover, but with diamond-shaped leaves. It grew in large patches that could be easily grazed on by bison or collected by humanoids. It tasted similar to oregano, except more bitter.

Usage
Consuming the leaves of Diamond Cure could counteract the effects of most snake venom, and could be used both to reduce the discomfort of minor bites and to save lives in the case of serious ones. It was recommended as a natural remedy for bites from both reed snakes and dead grass snakes.

While it was most potent when fresh, it retained some potency if harvested and dried thoroughly.

## Dienje Xavarri

Dienje Xavarri was a priestess of Iyachtu Xvim active in the Thayan city of Bezantur in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
Dienje was fanatical in her devotion to Iyachtu Xvim and dedicated every aspect of her life in his name.

Activities
She was the high priest and overseer of the House of Iyachtu Xvim following its construction in the 1360s DR. This was the grandest temple to her god in all of Thay, and a result, she was also the most powerful member of the Thayan church of Iyachtu Xvim, second only to the god himself. While Xvim was known to visit the House from time to time, Dienje was in charge of day-to-day operations of the temple and faith.

Consistent with her god's desire to usurp the powers of Cyric, Dienje was embroiled in a conflict between her followers and the Cyricists at the nearby House of Cyric.

Rumors & Legends
It was said that Dienje was not only Xvim's faithful priestess, but his lover as well. Some believed that the pair had at least one child, although it was unknown whether such an offspring would possess divine power or what kind of destiny a child of Xvim—and grandchild of Bane—might posses.

The festival occurred within the Sea Ward and North Ward of Waterdeep. Participants occupied Heroes' Walk, Heroes' Garden, and the street from Skulls Street all the way to the Street of Glances and the Street of the Singing Dolphin.

As most of Siamorphe's followers were composed of nobility, the vast majority of participants in the Divine Pageantry were nobles. Participating nobles dressed in old-fashioned costumes and tossed coins, usually in the form of copper or silver, to spectating commoners. The nobles spoke in stilted speech and addressed each other with full titles during the Divine Pageantry.

## Dirlagraun

Dirlagraun  was an aged displacer beast that traveled across the planes of existence with the Witchlight Carnival.

Personality
Dirlagraun spoke in a calm manner and was quite intuitive of the feelings of others.

Abilities
The mighty beast was granted the ability to speak and understand several languages spoken by mortals, including the common tongue.

Possessions
She wore a massive set of artificial butterfly wings attached to her tendrils and a collar around her neck that held a large keg of toffee apple juice. This juice was beloved by children and had a soothing effect when consumed.

Activities
Dirlagraun spent her days diligently serving as nanny to any children that found themselves lost within the Witchlight Carnival. She considered this charge to be her most important duty in life.

Relationships
Dirlagraun was raised by a family of noble high elves of the Summer Court.

The displaced beast once had her own cub named Star, before it inexplicably went missing from the carnival.  Dirlagraun never forgave herself for this loss.

## Diderius

Diderius was a powerful wizard that discovered an ancient and powerful divination pool.

Description
While the exact specifications are unknown, Diderius held some connection to the empire of ancient Netheril and possibly the survivor-state of Anauria. This as evidenced by the artistic works within his tomb, and the historical origins of one of his most treasured possessions.

Personality
Diderius held a great passion for divination and it was use of that magic that led to the pool that further enhanced his powers.

He was not overly greedy and offered his services to the townsfolk that lived near his mansion for meager compensation.

Abilities
During his life, Diderius used his divination pool to view worlds and planes of reality that never came into existence.

Possessions
One of his most prized possessions was a Netherese book entitled Transubstantiality across Potentialities.

Relationships
Diderius had at least one known apprentice, a young woman named Ilda who absolutely revered the old wizard. Unfortunately the two had a tragic falling out when Diderius came to believe that Ilda stole a valuable book from his collection.

He was known to have a large contingent of followers, and at least two warriors were known to have pledged their eternal existence to Diderius. They watched over his remains as wraiths for years after their deaths.

History
After his death, Diderius' followers converted an area of his mansion into a crypt for the old wizard. There, Diderius was entombed as a mummy lord, though neutral-aligned and in full possession of his faculties and wizardly spellcasting abilities. Four of his closest advisers were also mummified and posted as guards for their master, though this did not prevent the back chambers of the tomb from being colonized by a tribe of yuan-ti and their lizardfolk slaves many years later.

## Divine inspiration

Divine Inspiration was a divination spell that priests could use to gain knowledge of events happening in the near future.

A more powerful version of genius, this spell granted the caster knowledge regarding the near future. It had strict limitations, for example it could predict how sentries would react to a disturbance but it couldn't predict the issue of a battle. As for other similar spells, the granted knowledge was always relevant and correct, although it could be incomplete or cryptic.

This spell had a cool-down period of one day before it could be cast again.

A gem with a value of at least 500 gp was required to cast this spell, in addition to verbal and somatic components.

Divine Inspiration was one of the spells priests could learn by reading Wythyndle's Round Book.

## Dispel magic field

The dispel magic field was a spell used against Tsjancy Yildree during his time as the Magister.

This variant of dispel magic created a field of similar effects to it, but which instead "waited", and could not be removed with anything less than a limited wish.Presumably, the spell unleashed the effect whenever it encountered certain magic in range. It is assumed to be an abjuration spell for the purposes of this wiki. Its range, however, was limited.

The spell was used to disable Tsjancy Yildree, and then to keep him captive in a cell. The Magister finally bypassed the spell by severing the hand trapped in the manacle that kept him in place, then staggering out of the spell's range.

## Dire horse

Dire horse were bigger and wilder relatives of normal horses notable for their aggression and sheer strength.

Description
The beasts appear as huge versions of normal wild horses with wild, unkempt manes and bone plates growing out of the sides of their heads. Their hooves had deadly jagged edges.

Abilities
Powerful beasts, dire horses could carry loads of up to  and drag even more, up to . They had a keen sense of smell, capable of detecting foes and use it for tracking.

Combat
When confronted, dire horses tended to stomp at the enemies with their jagged hooves and with a bite right after.

Ecology
Dire horses could be found solitarily and in herds of six and up to thirty beasts. Being completely wild animals, dire horses were extremely hard to domesticate or tame due to their aggressive bestial nature.

Dire horses were known to inhabit the lands of Shaar, Murghôm, and Semphar. Their fierce nature and fearsome power led to several cults sprouting to worship the creatures, as well as numerous legends and folktales, and ghost stories were weaved about the dire horses of these regions.

Notable
Yeenoghu, the Demon Prince of Gnolls, was known to ride dire horses.

## Dispel shunt

Dispel shunt was a spell that protected its caster from dispel magic.

Effects
The spell remained in effect for ten minutes, redirecting any dispel magic spell cast upon the caster to another location of their choice about  or maybe about twice that. However, it would be reflected by any spell barrier at least as complex as a wall of ice. There was a risk, if the dispelling attempt was focused on its original caster, that the shunt would be broken instead, though it still protected the caster.

The shunt had unique interactions with antimagic field, being able to break that spell, but at the same time, it would be broken rather than suppressed; it had a similar response to Mordenkainen's disjunction, though its chances were somewhat slimmer. It also protected the target somewhat against a rod of cancellation, improving their odds of enduring the effect somewhat; finally, it could block the ray of a wand of negation, disabling it for a couple minutes due to feedback, though this consumed the shunt.

Components
The spell only required verbal and somatic components.

History
Azuth made this spell available through Elminster sometime prior to .

Any enemies of the caster within the area of effect felt the oppressive nature of the magic, hindering their combat abilities and their ability to resist the effects of other spells and magic. A dire chant lasted as long as the caster continued to chant. ## Disbelief

Disbelief was a divine enchantment spell from the thought sphere that allowed the caster to convince themselves that objects or creatures did not exist.

Effects
The caster could use this spell to temporaily convince themselves that objects or creatures within the area of effect did not exist, meaning that they could not interact in any way with the caster for the spell's duration. On the flip side, the caster could also not interact in any way with the same objects or creatures. If the caster received physical harm from the disbelieved subjects in reality while the spell was active, then the caster would experience one eighth to one half of that harm when the spell expired.

Components
This spell required only verbal and somatic components to cast.

History
The Scepter of Mystra, a relic of the Mystran faith, could cast the disbelief spell.

## Divine curse

Divine curse was a divine abjuration spell that affected a target with a terrible curse.

Effects
The immense evil power of a divine curse could only be used against someone who had committed a great act of evil that affected the caster. The caster selected the specifics of the curse, so their wording when casting the spell needed to be precise. A divine curse could only be removed by an atonement spell or if the wording of the spell left some kind of escape clause.

Components
In addition to verbal components, this spell required as material components the priest's holy symbol and a silver gong to cast.

History
Myrkyssa Jelan, a warlord in the Vast during the 14 century DR, had her ability to wield magic taken away by a divine curse.

## Divine arbiter

Divine arbiters were specialty priests devoted to Osiris, the Lord of the Dead within the Mulhorandi pantheon.

Activities
They possessed extensive knowledge of the laws and legal customs of Mulhorand and used that knowledge to aid with resolving disputes. Their equally impressive knowledge of necrology and the netherworld aided them in finding lairs of the undead and in using their weaknesses to their advantage if confronted. They could raise the dead, but only if they were convinced that their death was contrary to the law or maat.

Culture
Divine arbiters were always humans of Mulhorandi heritage.

Abilities
Given their close association with the netherworld, divine arbiters excelled at turning undead. They had access to arcane magic from the school of necromancy and were more resistant against death spells, eventually becoming completely immune to any energy-draining attacks from undead. They could cast spiritual hammer, which they referred to as "flail of deadly justice", once per day. Their animate dead spell was twice as effective as normal, though they only used this spell in defense of tombs or sacred sites. They could speak with the dead with any target regardless of how long ago they died provided they had the deceased's skull. Particularly powerful divine arbiters did not suffer any ill effects from casting resurrection or raise dead and those spells always succeeded.

Possessions
Divine arbiters wielded flails, warhammers, maces, staves, staff slings, or slings. They did not have any restrictions on the type of armor they could wear.

## Disir

Disir were unsightly slime-covered creatures found in the subterranean depths of Underdark's overpopulated areas, led by a disir queen.

Description
An average disir stood between  and, weighting around . The creature spent most of their lives hunched over, appearing shorter than their true height. Disir bodies were partially protected by natural chitinous armor while leaving parts of the rubbery green-white skin exposed. Disir constantly secreted green slime from the pores, coating their bodies in a gel-like substance. The slime collected dirt, dead flesh, and other rubbish in the wild, bringing forth a strong stench of decay.

The disir species did not have sexual dimorphism, making males and females appear virtually identical.

Abilities
The slime that coated disir's body and claws was poisonous but not deadly and when introduced into an open wound, caused immense pain. The infection of burning pain spread from the wound, and its intensity paralyzed affected muscles. The same thick poisonous coating protected the creatures from fire.

Combat

Disir's bite and claws were its main weapon; however, some were known to use weapons. These creatures preferred to rent their opponents with claws and only use bites to finish off enemies or to kill defenseless prey. Disir claws were powerful enough to crush stone, and their terrifying protruding jagged fangs, as well as razor-sharp tongues that had the texture of a rasp, were perfectly suitable to separate living flesh from bone.

Society
Disir colonies were rarely seen by inhabitants of Toril. Their communities had a tribal structure. Each tribe consisted of fifty or more closely associated disir, and more than one tribe could inhabit the same area. It was believed that disir tribes never came into conflict. However, they saw all other creatures and humanoids as food sources. Despite their high intelligence, disir never allied or negotiated with other species.

Disir were egg-laying species that changed their sex as they moved through life. Disir had a matriarchal society. Those disir who were in their female phase were the dominant individuals within the tribe. All duties were shared between the tribe's adults, including maternal tasks of nurturing and guarding tribe incubators where the disir eggs were laid, as well as disir larval young. Raids and hunts were done by all adults as well, and the food and other loot recovered from such raids were equally distributed among the tribe members. However, those adults who were unable to perform all of the tasks were killed.

Tribes were led by a disir queen and some had elite warriors, rogues, and shamans. Disir spoke their own language and could speak Common. Some also were known to speak Dwarvish, Goblin, or Ogrish.

Ecology
Disir could be found many miles under the planet's surface and were territorial creatures that felt uncontrollable hatred for all other creatures. Disir dug through their territory, leaving veering tunnels behind; however, more often than not, disir claimed homes and lair of others as their habitats. Due to shared terrain, disir were an enemy of tunneling creatures and cultures, such as dwarves and drow.

Despite being omnivorous creatures, disir preferred the taste of meat. They consumed fresh kills and rotting spoiled meat with equal delight. Among plant matter consumed with disir, mushrooms were the main food group. Knowledgeable subterranean travelers could detect disir presence by sterile dead remains the hunting parties left behind.

It took disir ten years to reach adulthood and the male stage of their lives. About half of these males later matured into females, ready for the breeding cycle or to take the queen's title. An average male lived for 30 years, while a queen could reach 60 years.

On Toril, disir inhabited the Underdark, where they tended to be left alone by the drow, who were too busy bickering and warring with eachother to turn against less common species found living nearby. Disir were more commonly found on the world of Krynn, while outside the Prime Material plane, these creatures were known to inhabit the Domains of Dread. On Krynn, disir were able to produce a slow-witted tyin offshoot who were adjusted to living on the surface.

Disir were among species that could cross-breed with certain types of dragons, producing disir hybrids.

Usages
Disir slime consul not be bottled to be used for its poisonous properties as it lost potency swiftly after being secreted and exposed to air.

## Dirty Dastard

The Dirty Dastard was a pirating vessel that was active in the seas around Chult during the late 15 century DR.

Description
After it was sunk, the Dirty Dastard's mast and upper decks were stripped away from the rest of the hull. The belly of the ship became overgrown with barnacles and sea snails.

History
About nine years before the death curse afflicted the Realms, the Dirty Dastard was sunk at sea. It washed ashore along the banks of the Snout of Omgar, and was looted by the tortles of Ahoyhoy shortly thereafter.

Some time later, the wreckage of the Dirty Dastard was taken over by the sea hag Myldryd Urchinspine and her two giant crab minions.

## Divinity Plays

The Divinity Plays were a series of performances performed and enjoyed by the Mulhorandi people on Midsummer each year. They were sponsored and organized by the churches of the realm.

Activities
Starting at dawn on Midsummer, actors throughout Mulhorand performed a series of plays that memorialized and celebrated their gods. The performances continued all day long until sunset.

Bards and other actors each portrayed the Mulhorandi gods―respectfully, but with humanity―as tradition dictated. The rigidity of Horus-Re's outlook on life was scoffed at by other deities; Set always orchestrated impractical schemes that failed for his goals, but wound up benefiting Horus-Re.

To the joy of many, the Red Wizards of Thay were depicted as spoiled, incompetent fools.

History
In the, a new performance entitled The Taking of Unther was added to the Divinity Plays. This play was a reenactment of the fall of Gilgeam and the salvation of the people from Unther.

## Diamond Staff of Chomylla

The Diamond Staff of Chomylla (or, occasionally, the Diamond Scepter of Chomylla) was one of the legendary lorestaves of Uvaeren, created to unlock the encrypted secrets of the ancient elven realm of Uvaeren.

Description
Measuring nearly  in length, the staff's thickness gradually tapered toward its base. As befitting its name, the Diamond Staff was crafted from a single diamond that had been magically elongated. Both the top and bottom of the staff were capped with six triangular, steeply tapering facets that converged to form pointed tips. The staff itself had six facets that ran along its full length, each of which was inscribed with nigh-invisible runes written in a forgotten elvish tongue. Around, the staff was described as being tipped with a transparent globe.

Although it was as light as a staff made from pine, the Diamond Staff was as durable as one made from adamantine. It was immune to item-destroying spells such as shatter.

The Diamond Staff emitted a strong aura of conjuration, divination, and illusion magic.

Powers
The main function of the Diamond Staff was to serve as a key to unlock the libraries and lore of Uvaeren. The Uvaeranni were a secretive people who encrypted and hid many of their secrets in countless relics and artifacts. When in proximity to such an encrypted secret, the Diamond Staff would emit a faint tingling sound audible only to its bearer, thus helping identify potential hidden lore. The staff could also be used to quickly gain a partial understanding of encrypted Uvaeranni lore.

Furthermore, the staff allowed its wearer to cast identify, legend lore, and tongues three times per day for each spell. Anyone in physical contact with the Diamond Staff could silently cast detect magic at will.

Should the staff's bearer choose to use it for the somewhat less dignified purpose of melee combat, it functioned as a +3 quarterstaff.

Finally, the Diamond Staff could be used to flood a target's mind with an overload of information, disabling them entirely. This effect was described as similar to the spell maze, or according to some sages, identical. Triggering this effect required the staff to make physical contact with the target.

History
The staff was created by Chomylla several centuries prior to the destruction of the elven realm of Uvaeren by a falling star in . Chomylla was among the few Uvaeranni to survive the catastrophe, as she was away visiting the court of Jhyrennstar on behalf of Mnuvae, the last coronal of Uvaeren. Before returning to her ruined homeland to determine what could be salvaged from its libraries, she left the Diamond Staff in the care of the Fourth Coronal of Jhyrennstar.The name of the Fourth Coronal of Jhyrennstar has not been identified in any source. Chomylla was never heard from again, and the Diamond Staff mysteriously disappeared from Jhyrennstar soon thereafter.

Around the time of the Weeping War (–), an orc of the Army of Darkness was said to have come into possession of the Diamond Staff. Although the N'Vaelahr of Cormanthor received intelligence reports indicating the orc could be found in the woods north of Oloriil, the staff was never recovered despite fears it could fall into yugoloth hands.

The staff's whereabouts remained shrouded in mystery for several centuries until the, when the green dragon Dretchroyaster uncovered it in the Lost Trench in the Darkwoods, which had been the site of one of the greatest battles of the Weeping War.

Some years later, in the, the Diamond Staff of Chomylla drew the interest of the adventuring troupe known as the Company of the Sunlit Sea. They tracked the staff to an abandoned temple of Bhaal in the forest of Cormanthor, where they were horrified to discover the lair of Dretchroyaster, now a dracolich, within. The company chose to brave the danger regardless, but were quickly forced to retreat when their wizard Imani was badly burned by a trap.

Eight years later, in the, Imani returned to the Dalelands to issue an open call for dozens of adventurers to finally recover the Diamond Staff. The Turmishan wizard had divined that Dretchroyaster and the Cult of the Dragon were attempting to overload the staff with eldritch energy in an attempt to access the lost lore of Uvaeren. Imani feared what would happen if these secrets should fall into evil hands, but also wanted to examine the lore of Uvaeren for himself. Under his direction, the adventurers who answered the call infiltrated Dretchroyaster's lair and retrieved the Diamond Staff.

With the Diamond Staff now in his possession, Imani soon organized an expedition to unlock a Uvaeranni library-vault, the Vault of Song, once again calling on the aid of adventurers. Unfortunately, the staff was stolen by the Fangfists, a local orcish tribe, before the expedition could even begin. Unbeknownst to all, the orcs had been hired to steal the staff by the Zhentarim mercenary Phoedele, who in turn had been hired by the Sembian wizard Barcere, who in turn was an agent of the Shadovar. Barcere, having heard rumors that the Diamond Staff had been recovered, wanted to use it to access Uvaeranni lore for his masters.

Notable Owners
 Chomylla, the staff's creator.
 The Fourth Coronal of Jhyrennstar, who Chomylla entrusted with the Diamond Staff shortly before her disappearance.
 Dretchroyaster, a green dragon turned dracolich who wanted to use the Diamond Staff to absorb the lost power of Uvaeren.
 Imani, a wizard with an interest in Uvaeranni lore.
 Barcere, another wizard with an interest in Uvaeranni lore.

## Din Caldwell

Din Caldwell was a member of the Caldwell patriar family of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
He was a short and balding man with a dark fringe of hair a mustache. He wore turquoise robes with a golden sash. He walked with the aid of a cane.

History
In the late 1490s DR, Lord Forge Fitzwilliam of Neverwinter revived the High Sun Games, a blood-sport in which adventurers would battle monsters in a maze in the newly built Neverwinter arena. Din Caldwell was one of a number of rich, powerful, and unscrupulous people who came to bet big on the Games and brought their riches with them. However, they needed a secure place to store their riches. To this end, Forge offered the use of Castle Never's treasure vault, but Din and the others wished to see its security for themselves. Thus, Forge his advisor Sofina showed Din Caldwell and Porb Piiradost around the castle, boasting of its upgraded security and suitability for storing their wealth in its vault, which was protected with a powerful Mordenkainen's arcane seal. Din laughed when Forge joked about the former lord who'd banned the Games, Dagult Neverember, being in a vegetative state. However, they were interrupted when Sofina sensed the presence of a wild shaped druid—Doric—spying on them, but she escaped Sofina and the castle guards.

Nevertheless, Din and the others trusted their wealth to Forge Fitzwilliam. This would prove to be unwise as Forge stole it all and, during the High Sun Games, tried to abscond the city with it. Meanwhile, Din and the others bet on the contestants and the monsters; Din idly asked Porb if he'd ever eaten displacer beast flesh, but Porb declared it too gamey for his liking. Din later bet '20,000' on the Gray Hands. Both their fortunes were later lost, scattered over the streets of Neverwinter for the common people to collect. But Din was not to know because, while he was watching and betting on the Games, Sofina revealed herself as a Red Wizard of Thay and used the horn of beckoning death, turning him and all the gamblers into zombies.

## Diana Cloppington

Diana Cloppington was an individual who worked at the Witchlight Carnival as a ticket-taker for the carousel.

Description
Due to a deal she made with a hag of the Hourglass Coven, Diana was cursed with a physical form that resembled a piebald centaur.

Personality
While she was personable and charming, Diana bore an inner sadness that became apparent over some time.

Abilities

A side effect of Diana's curse left physically incapable of discussing certain topics without retching up tree sap.

History
When she was still a human, Diana lost her beloved warhorse to some unknown fate. Hoping to be reunited with her steed, Diana ventured into the Feywild realm of Prismeer, and sought after Granny Skabatha Nightshade. Rather than bring Diana's horse back, Granny Nightshade fused Diana's body with that of her mount, transforming her into her form that so closely resembled a centaur.

After Diana ran away from Prismeer, she encountered Mister Light, one of two partners who ran the Witchlight Carnival. Mister Light felt for Diana's plight, and offered her both a job and home within the traveling Fey carnival.

## Diaries of Connel Bay

The Diaries of Connel Bay were a set of personal journals belonging to the warlock Connel Bay.

Description
These tomes described in great detail the ritual for entering into an infernal contract with Asmodeus, the same ritual that Connel Bay had undertaken long ago. They also contained detailed notes on powerful individuals whom Connel Bay had bribed into providing assistance to his nefarious schemes, as well as notes on various individuals in Faerûn that the warlock had subverted into entering pacts with Asmodeus. These included such people as Actilis of Rashemen, Dreveshnu of Amn, and Jard le Karn of Waterdeep.

The tomes had a magical nature to them, making their written word disappear before the eyes of any who looked upon them. Any attempt to cast a ritual upon them would also make the tomes lose their contents. Only by handling them with the proper magics could one feasibly read their contents.

History
Following his death, Connel Bay's diaries were buried with him in his tomb, kept within a chest guarded by many traps.

Around 1479 DR, a group of adventurers from Waterdeep tracked down the long lost Tomb of Connel Bay in Ganathwood and uncovered these diaries within. They went on to hand the books over to members of the Church of Oghma, one of the few groups at the time that could properly decipher their contents without destroying them in the process.

## Disorders of the Nerves and Mind: A Treatise

Disorders of the Nerves and Mind: A Treatise was a medical book that detailed various psychological and neurological ailments, their diagnoses, and related treatment plans, penned by the cleric-physician named Glenne Earl Mack.

Content
One case study included in Disorders of the Nerves and Mind shared the story of a woman, nicknamed "R," who was troubled by her husband, nicknamed "B," and his temper. According to the woman, just three months earlier, "B" was afflicted by restless dreams and night sweats. The husband screamed in his sleep about bearing the "mark of chaos." Two months before the physician's visit, "B" began calling himself Sarveok. And just a month before, "B" became obsessed with the throne he claimed he was destined to sit upon.

Glenne Earl Mack paid the couple's farmhouse a visit and met "B." The man simply sat at the farmers' table, holding a book close to his chest. Being a cleric as well as a physician, Glenne detected no possession, no magical influences, nor curse. When the cleric illuminated the afflicted man's face with her candle, "B" cried out, brandishing the book: "The deaths they bring shall awaken the father, and through them he will rise!" The cleric managed to grab the book and tossed it into the farmhouse's hearth. To the cleric's surprise, the tome burst into black flames and burned into ash. The book left behind nothing but a small scrap of paper that read: "He foresaw his coming death and seeded his essence across the land."

With the book gone, "B" seemingly woke from a nightmare but had no memories of the tome nor claiming to be Sarevok. In the aftermath of the incident, Glenne Earl Mack diagnosed "B" with an unspecified neurotic enthrallment and prescribed him a suspension of garlic and drace to be taken orally twice per day.

History
A copy of Disorders of the Nerves and Mind could be found in the libraries of the Emerald Grove in the .

## Dispel possession

Dispel possession was a spell that  granted temporary liberation to a targeted creature from the influence of certain magical effects.

Effects
When cast, this spell temporarily removed the influence of influence of charm spells, magic-based mental dominations and controls, psionic attacks or dominations, and ESP or similar eavesdropping magics. By utilizing this spell, a charm enchantment was permanently shattered, freeing the recipient from its influence. However, other forms of mental attacks and controls resumed their effect once the duration of the spell expires.

If dispel possession was directed at a victim who has fallen prey to a prior magic jar spell, the life force that had taken control of the victim's body was forcibly expelled and returned to its original container.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material component, a single teardrop from an elf, half-elf or human.

## Dip's Stagger

Dip's Stagger was a violent game of sorts enjoyed in the Cutter's Dip tavern in Yhaunn, Sembia, in the late 14 century DR.

Description
In the game, the patrons of the tavern would seize upon a vulnerable visitor who had earned their ire, and proceed to shove, hurl, and slam them about the tavern, cheering "Dip!" and "Stagger!" with each heave. The victim would be left bruised, dazed, confused, and soaked in cheap alcohol, unable to mount any sort of resistance to the constant strikes that bounced them across the room. The game would continue until the patrons grew bored, the victim escaped, or something else interrupted it.

History
A notable instance of the game was held in the, when the werewolf and priestess of Selûne Feena Archwood investigated the tavern for clues regarding the local cult of Shar. She had the misfortune to be recognized by the bandits Stag and Drik, who proceeded to assault her and drag her into a game of Dip's Stagger. Despite being an experienced champion of Selûne, Feena was left reeling and unable to raise any sort of defense, and couldn't even muster her will towards shapeshifting into a werewolf. She avoided whatever fate awaited her at the end of the game by chance, when the final "Dip!" sent her tumbling into the lap of the dissolute Sharran acolyte Keph Thingoleir, who proceeded to rescue her mostly out of idle contrarian opposition to her enemies.

## Dinjik

Dinjik was a technique for hunting foxes and wolves employed by the Iulutiun of the Great Glacier.

Description
A dinjik was specialized pit trap. It was a snowhouse constructed in the shape of a dome, standing approximately  tall. A square opening was carefully carved into the top and covered with delicate branches and a layer of snow. Within the snowhouse, enticing bait such as fresh seal meat was strategically placed. Tempted by the scent, a wolf or fox would unknowingly step onto the concealed opening, causing it to plummet into the snowhouse. The trapped animal found itself unable to leap out, as the height of the hole was insurmountable, and the curved walls prevented any escape by climbing.

## Divine shell

Divine shell was a defensive spell from the abjuration school of magic, used by divine spellcasters and silverstars of Selûne.

Effects
When the spell was uttered, the caster became surrounded by a maelstrom of magical energy and blocked and deflected attacks coming their way. Even if attacks managed to penetrate the barrier, their deadliness was significantly reduced, and the divine shell adsorbed it in part. It also absorbed part of any electricity, fire, cold, or acid attack. Additionally, divine shells made the casters completely impervious to missile attacks. Lastly, the caster's protections and reactions to various dangers, both physical and magical, were boosted and the spell allowed them a slight advantage in shaking off negative effects.

## Dimensional plane

Dimensional planes were pseudo-realities that permeated all the known planes of existence. Some considered them to be building blocks of reality that allowed for the coexistence of all the planes. The concept of these planes was typically beyond what the minds of most humanoids could fathom.

Description
The known dimensional planes included the Temporal Prime and spatial dimensions, with potentially others existing.

Types of Dimension Planes
Temporal Prime
The Temporal Prime was a dimension in which the various timestreams of the multiverse could be observed and used to move through time, i.e., time travel. Many sages on the Prime Material and even some chronomancers believed it to be a kind of demiplane within the Ethereal Plane, calling it the Demiplane of Time, trying to make fit it into their pre-existing notions and theories on the multiverse. However, many chronomancers swore that "dimensional plane" was the more technically correct term for the Temporal Prime.

Spatial Dimensions
The first three spatial dimensions were referred to as length, width, and depth or height. Some believed there were only up to five spatial dimensions, though far more existed.

The majority of lifeforms, such as humans and humanoids, existed in three-dimensional space and could only perceive the first three spatial dimensions. Some life forms could be found in higher dimensional spaces, such as the misi, which existed solely on the fourth through sixth spatial dimensions. Creatures of higher dimensional planes were often noted as not being able to see or interact with creatures of the lower dimensional planes.

With the right tools, powerful mages could open their perceptions to higher dimensional planes, such as Szass Tam, who created a nine-dimensional map of all then-surviving Dread Rings in Faerûn whilst wielding the crystal staff of the Zulkir Yaphyll in 1478 DR. However, this was an incredibly strenuous endeavor for even the most powerful of living or undead mages like Szass Tam, who collapsed from exhaustion shortly after dispelling his ninth-dimensional map. In addition, the rare potion of the fourth dimension allowed imbibers to temporarily enter and perceive the fourth dimension whilst retaining perception of three-dimensional space.

## Dizby

Dizby was a sailor and the quartermaster aboard the Lustrous Lass in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Dizby was a male wood elf with handsome and sharp features. He kept his head clean-shaven.

History
By the, Dizby was already thought to be old. His age and pessimistic outlook brought some mockery from his fellow sailors, who believed him to be a doom-sayer. Dizby was made quartermaster by Captain Harfang in or just before 1492 DR. This assignment disgruntled one of the Lustrous Lass's crew members – Jorkens, who wanted the position for himself. During the ship's anchorage at the Crone Island, the disgruntled sailor made a deal with the island hag, who, to no surprise, turned the deal against the entire crew of the Lass and a the same time, another deal was made by Captain Harfang. The captain was granted "enough gold to last the rest of her life," but soon enough, the sailor realized that the bag of gold contained only six real gold coins among the dross. As soon as she tossed the false gold aboard, the Captain came down with deadly sea plague that ended the lives of the entire crew in a bit over a tenday after the deal was made.

The bodies of the dead were left aboard the Lustrous Lass and quarantined, after she drifted into the harbors of Baldur's Gate.
