## Divine Empress Shrine

The Divine Empress Shrine was a shrine found in Miyama Province in Kozakura during the mid–14 century.

Description
The Divine Empress Shrine was steeped in legend, dating back approximately 2,000 years. It was once expansive, but has fallen into disrepair. Many of its peripheral structures were partially collapsed, and the surrounding wall was in ruins in several places. At the core of the compound stood the main hall of worship, encasing a large split boulder, symbolic of the legendary birth of the Divine Empress. Unfortunately, the building was in a dilapidated state, with a leaking roof and its once vibrant red pillars desperately required a fresh coat of paint. Among the six shrines dedicated to the Stone-Bearing-Empress scattered across Kozakura, this one was the least visited.

The shrine's upkeep was entrusted to three elderly men, former shukenja whose powers have waned. One was nearly deaf, another almost blind, and the third spent most of his time reminiscing about the past. Though friendly and well-meaning, they struggled with the infrequent presence of visitors, finding it difficult to accommodate their needs or recall the appropriate conduct in their company.

History
It was said that the Divine Empress, while rallying an army to confront the Oni Kings across Shinkoku, was pregnant. During her journey, the unborn child signaled its readiness to be born three times, but each time, she instructed it to wait. Upon reaching this particular location, the birth became imminent, and she delivered a stone. Disturbed by what she perceived as the child's disrespect in taking such a form, she commanded her soldiers to smash the stone. However, when struck, the stone shattered, revealing her son, Stone-Storm-Wind, fully grown. Despite the captivating tale, there was no concrete evidence to confirm the story or verify this shrine as the actual birthplace.

Recently, the three elderly shukenja have faced troubles from bakemono raiding parties and would welcome any assistance, provided they could effectively communicate their plight.

Possessions

History
In the, three new residents arrived in Lonelywood – three trappers who did not abide by the local ranger-protector's rules. To Emmerich Hawk's disdain, the brothers set out to trap forest beasts in the Lonely Wood without any regard for the damage they were doing to the forest. Digby and his brothers only care about skinning and selling as much as possible. Emmerich Hawk attempted to dissuade the trappers from their harmful practices, but the brothers considered the ranger to be just a crybaby who loved animals a bit too much. When a barbarian army approached Lonelywood in 1281 DR, Digby was not worried for the town's well-being; instead, he was happy to see all sorts of forest crutters being spooked by the barbarians and being sent running straight into the brothers' traps.

## Divine miasma

Even the smallest shards of bones from dead deities giant bodies floating in the Astral Sea sent hairs on the mortals' arms stand from the divinity contained inside. ## Dippin' soup

Dippin' Soup was one of the simplest soups made in the Realms and always served as an accompaniment to bread, rather than the other way around.

Description
This simple soup required just a small handful of ingredients. Firstly, tomatoes were skinned and mashed into a pulp. Then, chopped onions and smashed cloves of garlic were fried in lard. Then the tomatoes, fresh or dried herbs were added in and seasoned further with salt and pepper. The concoction was cooked for half and hour until it was thickened and perfect for dipping. It was recommended to remove onions and garlic from the soup to be eaten separately.

Dippin' Soup was eaten with hearty chunks of bread and cheese.

History
In the mid-to-late 15 century DR, a recipe of the Dippin' Soup was recorded and published in the Soups of the Realms by Brother Donnick, the priest cook from the Open Hand Temple dedicated to Ilmater, the Crying God, locataed in the Rivington district of Baldur's Gate.

## Diomira Trellis

Diomira Trellis was an Untaken of Umberlee, a low-ranking member of the Bitch Queen's clergy under Flood Tide Allandra Grey of the Water Queen's House in Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Diomira was a human woman with long straight black hair. She had heterochromia – Diomira's left eye was bright aquamarine, and her right eye was black in color.

History
Circa the, during the Absolute crisis, Untaken Diomira Trellis was among the low-ranking members of the who were not allowed to perform the ceremonies and sing hymns in praise of the Bitch Queen. She quietly prayed at the temple whilst Flood Tide Allandra Grey and other priestesses sang and performed a fellow priestess's Holli Dylford's last rites. The recently perished priestess died while swimming in Baldur's Gate's Gray Harbor.

## Dire shark

Dire sharks were a kind dire animal that were an unusually large and ferocious type of shark.

Description
A dire shark had a toothy mouth set well under its long snout with a streamlined body and a triangular fin atop its back, and a crescent moon shaped tail. They were up to 25 feet (7.5 meters) long and could weigh more than 20,000 pounds (9,000 kilograms) thus being the second-largest type of shark to swim the oceans, only superseded by the megalodon.

Behavior
Encountered as either solitary specimens or in schools of up to five members, they would attack anything they perceived as edible no matter how large it was.

Abilities
A dire shark had a keen sense of smell that it used to hunt down prey in the great oceans. It would attempt to swallow whole anything of lesser size than itself, and would employ an aggressive hit-and-run tactic against its prey.

Ecology
Habitats
Dire sharks were native to the Fated Depths, a Fiendish Plane in the World Tree cosmology model."

Summoning
A dire shark ally could be summoned with the spells summon nature's ally VIII and summon monster IX.

History
In the late 14 century DR, a priest of Umberlee sent a fiendish dire shark to attack coastal towns in Luiren that refused to pay tithes to the evil goddess of the seas.

Sometime after the Spellplague rich adventurers could buy a dire shark mount for the vast sum of 21,000 gold pieces, such a mount would allow the rider to ride some  every day in the water.

## Diamssar

Diamssar was a representative of the Triceratops Society in Port Nyanzaru in Chult during the late 15 century DR. Secretly, he was actually a spy for a group of yuan-ti.

Description
He had pale skin and dressed in dark clothes.

Personality
Hiss skills with deception hid his true fanatical nature.

Possessions
He was in possession of a potion of healing and a jeweled dagger.

Relationships
Diamssar worked with the Harper agent Soggy Wren, whom he had been manipulating into finding the pieces of a Dreamer's Amulet that had been smuggled into Port Nyanzaru by his yuan-ti masters. He was affiliated with Lekhet, a yuan-ti pureblood.

He had a pet giant poisonous snake.

History
During the events of the death curse around 1490 DR, Diamssar was a double agent for a group of yuan-ti while working as a representative of the Triceratops Society. For the Triceratops Society, he worked outside the public bathhouse. His secret yuan-ti business operated out of the abandoned Northside Market Textile House warehouse in the Market Ward.

## Dizzy Drake

The Dizzy Drake was a tavern in the Lower Trade Lanes of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
The Dizzy Drake was a modest one-story building with cellars beneath in fair yet fading condition.

Description
Once a lively establishment, as of 1370 DR it suffered from a dwindling clientele, many patrons having been frightened off by unsettling rumors of disappearances around the tavern. The tavern’s proprietor, Choren Lendoren, a human male and former fighter, has become noticeably lax in his management. He has developed the habit of drinking alongside his patrons, often forgetting orders or even engaging in arguments, a far cry from his former professionalism. For the few patrons who continued to frequent the establishment, the offerings were satisfying, with reasonably priced drinks and bar snacks of average quality, recently marked down in an attempt to attract business.

Choren’s declining behavior, however, was rooted in a grim and secret fear. Unbeknownst to most, the yuan-ti lich Nhyris D'Hothek has taken refuge in hidden tunnels beneath the Dizzy Drake’s cellars. A concealed door, cleverly disguised behind a row of casks, lead directly to these subterranean chambers. Choren, aware of this malevolent presence, was deeply afraid of the lich and has reluctantly complied with its demands. Under threat, he has begun leading unsuspecting patrons into the cellars to satisfy Nhyris's dark whims.

Despite considering abandoning the tavern, Choren remained paralyzed by fear of the lich’s shadowraths. Haunted by the prospect of being transformed into a shadowwrath or facing some equally horrific punishment, Choren felt trapped, bound to his failing tavern and to the bidding of the creature lurking below.

## Dinosaur races

The dinosaur races of Port Nyanzaru in Chult were one of that city's most famous attractions as of the late 15 century DR.

Description
The races featured brightly painted dinosaurs ridden through the streets of Port Nyanzaru for the delight and gambling of onlookers. Racing dinosaurs were usually juveniles (as they were easier to train and manage) and usually had blunted claws and horns in addition to strong muzzles. In some cases, riders of smaller species were preferred to make smaller dinosaur runners competitive. Minor usage of magic or of magic items was generally permitted during races.

Races were held once each tenday, and a day of races could last well into the evening. When night fell or if fog rolled in during the races, torches were set up to mark the route. Each race day usually included three events: a four-legged race (dominated by ankylosaurus, triceratops, and dimetrodon racers), a two-legged race (dominated by deinonychus and hadrosaurus racers), and an "unchained" race with no entry restrictions (dominated by allosaurus and tyrannosaurus racers). "Unchained" races also differed from the other events in that the dinosaurs were permitted to attack each other and did not need to be muzzled nor have their claws and horns trimmed, making these events much more dangerous for the dinosaurs and their riders. However, spectators were seldom injured.

Special events could also include chariots or races through the Harbor (astride plesiosaurs) or through the air (on pteranodons). On occasion, small races were held within the Grand Coliseum as part of larger events: these usually featured smaller allosauruses and riders who were encouraged to taunt, trip, and attack each other as they navigated around obstacles like blockades, mud pits, and nets.

Location
A usual racecourse started either at the Harbor or at the Tiryki Gate in the Market Ward. It wound through the streets of Port Nyanzaru passed the Harbor and around all four of the city's main hills (Mount Sibasa, Yklwazi Hill, Temple Hill, and Throne Hill).

The area of the Market Ward just inside the Tiryki Gate was a gathering place for bookies and gamblers.

Activities
Racing was managed by teams, who were known to recruit from among daring adventurers as the races could be quite dangerous. Such teams were usually named by a convention of combining a color with a native Chultan animal, e.g., the Green Vipers. Other teams were sponsored by important individuals, such as Pock-Marked Po's team, the Bladefangs.

Betting was managed by bookies, who accepted wagers of as little as a single copper piece and as large as 500 gold pieces. Gamblers could bet on a given dinosaur winning, placing, showing, or doing something specific during the race, such as attacking another racer or losing its rider. This meant that bookies would accept many different bets on a single race, and winnings were paid out based on the odds. Losing bettors were expected to pay their gambling debts promptly or else be visited by a "legbreaker".

History
As of the time of the death curse, crowd favorites at the races included Banana Candy, Big Honker, Bonecruncher, and Ubtao's Favorite. Less popular racers included Grung Stomper, Jungle Princess, Mountain Thunder, Nasty Boy, and Scarback.

During this time, the Merchant Princes of the city arranged for a massive competition called Na N'buso's Haka, which featured land, air, and sea events all in one race. In the lead up to this festival, there was a suspicious uptick in longshot wins, mostly due to a number of dinosaur racers dying mysteriously.

## Dinosaur pens

The dinosaur pens of Port Nyanzaru, Chult, were paddocks where most of the city's dinosaurs were housed and trained.

Location
The pens were located in the Tiryki Anchorage district outside the walls of Port Nyanzaru proper. They lay at the southeastern outskirts of the city, but were close enough that the harsh smells and throaty noises of the beasts made their way into the Anchorage. This location also meant that the dinosaurs served as an early-warning system as they would sense the approach of danger from the jungle.

Structure
The smelly pens included both cages and muddy enclosures surrounded by  palisades to keep the dinosaurs separated by species, although some of these walls were only barely strong enough to hold back the creatures they separated. The fences were adorned with decorative Chultan shields, colorful banners, and torches to keep the area well-lit at night. Snaking bamboo catwalks atop the walls allowed trainers to keep an eye on hungry dinosaurs from a safe height.

Activities
The pens were the area where Port Nyanzaru's beasts of burden and racing dinosaurs alike were trained and housed at night. Most common among these dinosaurs were ankylosaurus, deinonychus, hadrosaurus, and triceratops, followed by allosaurus and dimetrodon, as well as the occasional prized stegosaurus or tyrannosaurus.

The animal handlers at the pens were highly skilled and worked with the same animals each day. They sometimes used long poles tipped with with blunt iron hooks to corral the dinosaurs. These trainers worked for the merchant prince Ifan Talro'a, who controlled both the sale and the training of all beasts within Port Nyanzaru. He also held authority over the entirety of the pens.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, Ifan Talro'a maintained a small stone bunker somewhere beneath the northern side of the pens. He used this "office" to manage communications with his mysterious allies from deep in the jungle by safeguarding written messages, storing supplies, and keeping flying snake messengers. The entrance was a locked metal door hidden amongst the pens, and the interior was guarded by conjured shadows and shadow demons. A member of the Emerald Enclave named Screaming Wind learned about his activities and strove to discover this office to find the proof.

In the lead-up to the inaugural day of a special dinosaur race known as Na N'buso's Haka, a series of dinosaur racers became mysteriously ill. On the day of the event, it was discovered that racers were being poisoned in their pens with a drug known as Wanderlost.

## Divine mind

A divine mind was a psionics wielding holy warrior, who channeled their control of the Invisible Art through faith and commitment to the ethics and values of their chosen god. As psionic paragons of their belief, these warriors were able to distill their passion towards a particular universal concept a god represented into a psionic mantle of thematic powers. They were akin to a paladin among the various types of psionics wielders.

Culture
Across the Realms, divine minds were comparatively far fewer in number than the more common psionic pathways of the psion, psychic warrior, soulknife, and wilder. Beyond Toril, they could be found on the worlds of Eberron and Oerth.

While they were most frequently encountered on their own, a recluse in the worst of cases, divine minds could often be found living in organized religious structures, such as a monastery or temple.

The alignment of a divine mind could fall anywhere within the spectrum. They were generally composed and even-tempered folk, not lusting for power or viewing themselves as the leader of any group they were in. Nor did they conceitedly thinking they (or their church) should have the majority of treasure from any adventure or quest they were in. Much like paladins, with their code of conduct or personal oath, divine minds swore themselves to the requisite ethics of their deity's alignment. They lacked the self-righteous disdain that often plagued paladins and their interactions with lay folk, though would speak up if they felt a particular deed was against the dictate or philosophy of their chosen faith.

Divine minds were equally as likely to come from any of the psionically-inclined sentient races, but humans seemed most apt to follow such a path. Savage and monstrous humanoids generally lacked the necessary studies to become divine minds.
Religions
Divine minds as a whole were not found to lean towards any one deity in particular, though it was not uncommon for them to show favor towards deities whose portfolio included War, such as Bane, Torm, or Tyr. While some felt a calling from a particular god, many chose a philosophy to follow and then a god that matched it. And whichever deity they chose to devote themselves to, it was usually one who would not be opposed to their alignment.

One could find a divine mind dedicated to any one of the deities of the Faerûnian pantheon, as well as Corellon Larethian, Garl Glittergold, Gruumsh, Moradin, Yondalla, and even Vecna. However, they were most frequently found among religions with a specific connection to psionics, such as Deep Duerra, Deneir, Ilsensine, Laduguer, Pisaethces, and Sseth. During the time of Jhaamdath, divine minds within the empire typically worshiped Auppenser, and some could still be found worshiping the god in the late 14 century DR.

Compared to divine spellcasters, paladins, and priests, they were more detached from the religion they followed, for rather than revering the sanctity of the god, a divine mind revered the concepts and philosophies that deity represented. This resulted in them often being beneath a god's notice. Nevertheless, many divine minds considered themselves to be representatives of their chosen deity, whose presence in any adventure or quest legitimized it as holy.

Some divine minds who revered either Talos or Umberlee took their devotion to the concept of storms to greater heights, becoming what were known as storm disciples.

Relationships
Being fervid warriors of faith, divine minds found the most joy in dealing with other members of their faith, such as clerics and paladins. However, they would enthusiastically adventure with with anyone those whose powers and proficiencies supplemented their own.

Abilities
Divine minds, like ardents, drew their psionic powers from psionic mantles, distillations of universal concepts and philosophies. Divine minds mentally distilled the essence of one attribute from their chosen deity's portfolio to create and adorn themselves with a mantle, a framework of mental desire. Overtime they drew more powers from their chosen mantle as their faith and mental strength grew, until eventually a divine mind could draw upon other aspects of their deity's portfolio, learning a second and even third psionic mantle.

In addition to psionic powers, divine minds could channel the welfare of their god into a unique psionic ability when psionically focused. The effects and benefits of this mantle power varied widely depending upon the psionic mantle that the divine mind followed. Additionally, all divine minds began their journey with some form of wild talent, granting them initial access to their mantle power.

The most distinct power of the divine mind was that of the psychic aura. All divine minds could express a basic defensive, offensive, or perception-based aura, in addition to a specialized aura derived from their divine mantle. These auras affected them and their allies, or in some cases their enemies. For novices the psychic aura had a radius of, but as they progressed over time its area of coverage would grow longer, with masters expressing psychic auras that were . Novices could only express one psychic aura at a time, while the more experienced could express two, and the masters of their field were capable of having three active at a time.

Just as a paladin could be deprived of their divine abilities if they ever violated the code of conduct laid down by his or her patron, particularly if they showed no sign of repentance, so too could a divine mind lose their mantle powers and abilities. Once "fallen," a divine mind could not regain their mantles without atoning for their transgressions in an appropriate manner.

Beyond these various powers and abilities, divine minds were often skilled in the arts of autohypnosis and psicraft. In battle they were exceptionally capable and flexible martial combatants, equally capable with most armors and weapons, though would never be found wielding tower shields and tended to lean towards melee weaponry.

## Dire polar bear

Dire polar bears were massive and deadly predators, bigger, stronger, and naturally armored, unlike their smaller kin.

Description
These ursines looked similar to their smaller cousins: regular polar bears. However, they were the size of a small house, with their backs armored with tough bony ridges. Their black claws were as sharp as swords. Even compared to dire bears, dire polar bears were the largest of the family, reaching  in length on average. An adult specimen weighed as much as .

Behavior
Dire polar bears were aggressive beasts that hunted and stalked prey: travelers, adventurers, and explorers.

Abilities
Like their smaller kin, dire polar bears were fast swimmers and possessed a keen sense of smell that aided them during the hunt. Due to their white coats, they also remained undetected among white snow dunes.

Combat
Not intelligent enough for anything but a straight assault, a dire polar bear relied on its sharp claws in battle. It pummeled and rended its victims. If a dire polar bear got close to its prey, it attempted to hug them, crushing and ripping them apart with its maw.

Ecology
Dire polar bears could be found in the coldest arctic regions, hunting in the snow-swept plains, either as solitary predators or in mated pairs. Dire polar bears occasionally descended into the Icewind Pass in Icewind Dale from their primary habitat in the Reghed Glacier. They were also known to be tamed by a bigger and more dangerous species found in the North: frost giants.

Outside of the Prime Material plane, fiendish dire polar bears inhabited the glaciers of Cania, the 8 layer of Baator.

## Diamond Legion

The Diamond Legion was a longstanding civic organization of the city of Procampur that served as both its city watch and military for over a millennium, becoming a higly trained and professional fighting force that was the envy of other city-states in Faerûn.

Organization
Most citizens of Procampur spent at least two, but often times up to four, years in service to the city as a member of the Diamond Legion. The organization had no gender restrictions, allowing many women to join and serve with distinction.

The Diamond Legion was divided up into several branches, including naval, air, artillery support, magical support, and a Reserve Corps. Their air division was known as the Eagle Riders.

The Legion included a number of ranks, with such titles as Lieutenant, Captain, Sergeant Captain, Sergeant Major, and the leading title Chief of Staff. Officers as low as lieutenants had the authority to deputize citizens.

Activities
The Diamond Legion was responsible for maintaining law across all of Procampur, maintaining vigilance against any form of crime. Starting around the 1360's DR,Pirate of Hearts gives Alaric Grimm's "present" age as 33 and the rough date of his debilitating injury as in his "late twenties". Stop Spreading the News states in its opening that the events of Pirate of Hearts had occurred nine months prior. As the former is presumed to be set in 1371 DR, then it can be extrapolated that this occurred sometime around the 1360s. the Legion ceased to have oversight over the city's Nobles District, with it falling under the exclusive purview of the newly formed Guardians of the Star Sapphire. This state of affairs was reversed around 1372 DR with the disbanding of the Guardians.

The Diamond Legion closely monitored travel between Procampur's districts. Out of all the districts, they were most present in the Port District.

If a Legionnaire ever suffered a debilitating injury that would interfere with their line of work, they would be given an honorable discharge.

One of the least glamorous duties of the Diamond Legion was to join the city's Dreaded Harbinger in parading criminals through the public streets. They did so in groups of eight, with two holding the chains that bound the prisoner.

Tactics
Legionnaires were noted for upholding a courteous demeanor to both Procampans and visitors alike, though they would react accordingly if they ever felt that their kindness was being taken for granted. Due to their diligence they would often stop citizens they didn't recognize or just found suspicious.

Those operating in the Port District had a tendency to look the other way when it came to matters that didn't concern Procampur or its citizens. Whenever fights broke out in the district, unless the combatants were armed with weapons Legionnaires wouldn't intervene, but instead let the brawl run its course. Then once all those involved in the brawl were tuckered out, the Legionnaires would move in to apprehend them.

Base of Operations
The organization was based in several Diamond Legion Branch Offices, or LBOs, across Procampur. One was located near the entrance of each city district. For districts with two entrances, such as the Port District, there were two offices. Additionally, the Port District Tower served as a station for the Legion. And they had a special barracks in the Castle District.

Relationships
The Legion worked closely with the city's Proctors in investigating crimes and mediating simple conflicts.

The organization's relations with the Procampan public were quite well. Older children and young men were strongly encouraged to join up as squires, as Procampans viewed service as a right of passage into adulthood, a place where one could learn of duty and responsibility. Men who failed to serve time in the Legion were seen as irresponsible, to both themselves and their community, as well as unfit to raise a family. As for Procampan women, many young women who did not join up with the Diamond Legion itself instead volunteered at programs that helped to maintain it.

History
The Diamond Legion had been around as an institution in Procampur since circa -230 DR, back when it was the site of Proeskampalar.The first three cited sources state that the Diamond Legion had been around for 1600 years. Assuming that the Present Age of that material was 1370 DR, this would put the organization as far back as -230 DR. However, according to other sources, like Forgotten Realms Campaign Setting, the human settlement of Procampur only dates back to 523 DR. Assuming that 1600 years of service is accurate, that would imply that the organization traces its lineage back to an organization in Proeskampalar, an ancient dwarven town that once occupied the same site. This hypothesis is somewhat supported by the adventure To Kill a Nightingale, where on p.11 a Legion Branch Office building is described as having "strong dwarven influences that most likely dates back to its original construction long, long ago." Going as far back as the proper founding of Procampur in 523 DR, the Diamond Legion's office corp included members of House Grimm, one of the city's founding noble houses. Over the next millenia, the Legion would develop a reputation for being nearly undefeated.

Around the, the city-state of Sembia attacked Procampur and tried to siege its walls with a plentitude of siege ladders, catapults, and trebuchets. During this conflict almost the entirety of the Diamond Legion were stationed on the city's walls.Stop Spreading the News states in its opening that the events of Pirate of Hearts had occurred nine months prior. As the former is presumed to be set in 1371 DR, then it can be extrapolated that the attack from Sembia mentioned in Pirate of Hearts to have taken place 14 years prior must have happened in 1357 DR.

Around the 1360's DR, the well respected and competent young officer Alaric Grimm led a rescue mission into an mine shaft on the outskirts of Procampur. The tunnel proved to be unstable and collapsed upon the company of Legionnaires, trapping them there for several days. By the time he was rescued Alaric had lost 20% mobility in his left leg and peripheral vision in his left eye, both of which were too far gone for restorative magic to fix. Alaric received an honorable discharge and bitterly moped for the proceeding few months. Then one day he discovered a scroll within the Grimm family library that was a charter for the Guardians of the Star Sapphire, a defunct private guard for Procampur's Nobles District. He brought this to his father, who then approached the Thultryl Rendeth of the Royal Blood about reviving the group. On the advice of his Hamayarch, Alamondh, the Guardians were commissioned to guard the Noble's District, with Alaric acting as their commander.

Around the month of Flamerule, in the, a plague broke out in Procampur's District of the Poor and the Diamond Legion's Reserve Corps were activated to contain it.Stop Spreading the News features a diary entry dated Flamerule 13, 1370 DR. However, a blurb on page 6 states that the Faerûnian pantheon is busy because the "Trial of Cyric" is going on, which would place these events in 1371 DR.

Some months after this, a failed assassination attempt on Thultyrl Rendeth led to the outbreak of another plague. With the city's ruler out of commission, Hamayarch Alamondh was forced to become Acting Thultyrl. In his first act as Thultyrl, Alamondh disbanded the Diamond Legion, arguing that they had failed in their job at protecting the Thultyrl and in keeping Procampur free of Sembian agents. This decision struck Procampans by surprise. Following their disbanding, the Legion's commanding officers went missing. In the first month without their diligent patrols, crime surged in the city and the new plague was running rampant in the streets. Then on an early afternoon in the month of Uktar, Alamondh decreed from the balcony of Thultyrl's Castle that the Guardians of the Star Sapphire were to be charged with filling the void, earning great applause at the time from the beleaguered citizenry. This newly expanded law enforcement agency acted as Alamondh's instruments in instituting his authoritarian regime, conducting mass arrests of both criminals and any opposition to Alamondh's rule, cracking down on both the Fraternity of Venturers and Society of Entertainers and Providers as alleged dissident hotbeds of criminal activity. These recriminations would be used to further justify the disbanding of the Diamond Legion over alleged incompetence.

The Thultyrl's fiance, Alissa Wyndsong, sometime after being branded a criminal by Alamondh broke into Thultyrl's Castle and ferried her ailing lover away to the city of Ravens Bluff, hoping with proper care and supervision he could recover. Eventually, the sword of Thallastam was discovered and hidden inside of it was found to be a cure to Rendeth's illness. At last free of his illness, Rendeth led the remaining loyalists of the Diamond Legionnaires in an assault against Procampur to retake the crown. Eventually their efforts win out and they discover that Hamayarch Alamondh had been dead for a long time, being no more than a puppet for the lich Cahoun.

With Rendeth returned to his place as the rightful Thultyrl of Procampur, he decreed that the Diamond Legion were to be reformed and the Guardians of the Star Sapphire were to be disbanded, with Legionnaires set to detain Star Guards for questioning. Additionally, all those who had been accused of crimes by the Guardians and imprisoned were to be pardoned. Rendeth initially offered Alaric Grimm reinstatement into the reformed Diamond Legion, along with the rank of Major, but the former leader of the Star Guards submitted himself to house arrest, hoping that the other members of his disbanded organization would follow his example and turn themselves in peacefully.

Notable Members
 Alaric Grimm.
 Alexander Breng, the Chief of Staff for the Diamond Legion for much of the 14 century DR.
 Augustus Grimm, father of Alaric Grimm.
 Bialtor Doogen, a former member of the Diamond Legion's naval branch.
 Llandra Skyrider, commander of the Eagle Riders in the 14 century DR.
 Melinde Piniago, a short-lived Legionnaire who was asked to leave after starting one too many fights
 Thalus Bailey.

## Dia Depthcaria

Dia Depthcaria was a half-orc sorcerer of Cormyr during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Dia had pale brown skin and long white hair.

History
Dia traveled along Cormyr's High Road around the time of the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar. At one point, she had a moment of trouble identifying a mysterious magical item she had found, seeking aid from passing adventurers.

## Dire bulette

Dire bulettes were an exceptionally huge variety of bulette.

Combat
Dire bulettes were heavily armored and attacked with their sharp teeth. They preferred to strike at their prey by burrowing beneath their feet and erupting up from below, sending a spray of rock and dirt into the air and leaving the ground destroyed and treacherous. They could move at frightening speed while burrowing just below the surface.

Ecology
Like ordinary bulettes, dire bulettes generally made their lairs below ground and were often solitary creatures. They would sometimes tag along with a gray render to exploit and scavenge in the wake of the destruction it caused. They were said to sometimes cooperate with giants or nabassu.

Homelands
They could be found in the Highlands of Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles. Local firbolg hunters could become celebrated for their skill if they successfully hunted dire bulettes.

They were said to be found in the Outer Planes within desert environments.

## Diamond Thimble

Diamond Thimble was a shop catering to adventurers in the city of Karador, capital of Sarifal on the Moonshae Isles. The proprietor was Omik Twede.

Services
The shop sold supplies but was better known for its armor, notably Omik's signature armor of etherealness.

## Diadree

Diadree was an old woman of Orunn in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
By, Diadree was gaunt and filthy, with a single braid of gray hair. She had a broken ankle that hadn't healed properly, and her blue eyes were milky.

Personality
Diadree was fiercely, proudly independant, and had a temper; despite being unstable, she behaved sufficiently grounded in reality.

Relationships
Diadree spoke at times to Amrennathed, an amethyst dragon.

History
Diadree lived in a house just on the outskirts of Orunn, a village in the Mountains of the Alaoreum, near a cliff overlooking Fox Ear lake. She had been old for at least as long as Bahrn, a local inhabitant, remembered, which meant she would have lived there since  at the very least, most likely; at some point while he was a kid, she'd broke a broom on his head.Bahrn being at least 18 years old in 1372 would suggest he'd been conscious and remembering people for at least 15 years, roughly when he was 3 years old.

During the passing of the great wyrm Amrennathed in the, Diadree was close enough that, when the dragon relinquished its secrets to the mountain, some of them became nestled in her mind. She remained in the mountain ever since, despite a severe foot injury and a new development of periodic tremors, because she held on to the hope that Amrennathed might return and take back her memories at some point.

After the same passing caused earthquakes that destroyed the village and forced its abandonment, Diadree remained at the site. The passing travelers she spoke to in the meantime, she told that she wanted to stay with Amrennathed; mentioning this name eventually drew the attention of the wizard Arlon, of the Cult of the Dragon, five years later.

Arlon hired Bahrn, who'd since become a mercenary, to guide him to Orunn. There, the two of them eventually managed to subdue Diadree. Arlon used his magic on Diadree to guide himself to Amrennathed's lair, leaving her and Bahrn behind. She talked Bahrn into chasing after the wizard, revealing he was from the Cult of the Dragon; though skeptical, he complied, carrying her to the lair.

She was injured in the fight that ensued, as Arlon drew the attention of Amrennathed's guardians and turned on her, but Bahrn defended her; afterwards, she asked to be left there, so that maybe in the presence of the dragon's remains, the parts of her mind that had stayed with her may fade from her memory. However, her injuries looked beyond severe, strongly implying that she was at risk of death.

Rumors & Legends
The mage Arlon falsely claimed Diadree was a witch, and his cousin, when seeking her out.

## Dilea Bromath

Dilea Bromath was a paladin of the Silver Flame in the world of Eberron.

Description
Dilea was a human woman with pale skin and a bob of black hair. She wore a full suit of chain mail and a white surcoat.

History
During the Darkening of Faerûn, Dilea was one of many who arrived to investigate the Spinner's Prison following the escape of the Spinner of Shadows. They found the Rift Between Worlds, a massive portal to the Demonweb Pits, and struggled to understand its nature while attempting to prevent it from threatening Eberron. Dilea correctly speculated that Lolth's domain was a realm of demons, and worked with Geras Pellim and Tanibur Evs to prepare a response.

## Dint

Battlemaster Dint was a Purple Dragon of Cormyr, who oversaw the town of Eveningstar during the invasion from Sschindylryn in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Dint was a heavily-built older man, with a long white beard. He wore a suit of chain mail with a white surcoat.

History
As one of the highest ranking officers in the realm, Battlemaster Dint personally joined the defense of Eveningstar when the drow of Sschindylryn attacked. He was fully open to hiring and rewarding adventurers in the fighting, in addition to recruiting them into the Dragons.
